--- GAP ANALYSIS ---
=== SURVIVABILITY GAP ANALYSIS ===
Cells where the link survives (Map B: recoverable/marginal) but the
threat itself is not neutralized (Map A: non-recoverable). This is the
regime where survival comes from trading link margin/rate, not from
removing the attack.

=== KPI #3: Decision Speed (DQN vs Rule-Based) ===

Loaded 9 threats (SNR-averaged) from results/closed_loop_diagnostic_results.mat

=== KPI #3: Decision Speed (DQN vs Rule-Based) ===
Generated: 21-Sep-2026 23:58:32

NOTE: "recovery cycles" is not a meaningful metric for this architecture --
every action applies a fixed one-shot dB mitigation and both policies are
deterministic, so both converge in exactly 1 decision regardless of policy.
The metric that actually differs is DECISION LATENCY (real, timed below).

DQN latency/recovery/agreement are averaged across the full SNR sweep
(each threat has one data point per SNR point in the diagnostic run).

Threat                       DQN (ms)      Rule (ms)     Agree%     Recov%
jamming                         1.401        0.00136       100%      85.7%
reactive_jamming                1.395        0.00014       100%      85.7%
sweeping_jammer                 1.392        0.00011        83%      60.5%
noise_burst                     1.475        0.00014         0%      71.5%
path_loss                       1.111        0.00021         0%      83.9%
spoofing                        1.395        0.00012         0%      83.5%
antenna_fault                   1.247        0.00015         0%      60.6%
benign_interference             1.093        0.00015       100%        N/A
none                            1.451        0.00016       100%        N/A

Mean DQN latency:  1.329 ms
Mean Rule latency: 0.00028 ms
Speed ratio: Rule-Based is ~4694x faster than DQN (lookup table vs neural net inference)
DQN-vs-Rule agreement (overall, all threats x SNR): 53.7%

CAVEAT: agreement compares only the chosen ACTION NAME between policies.
rule_based_policy.m's own per-threat mitigation_db values are computed but
never applied to any BER calculation -- both policies' physical mitigation
effect goes through the same action_mitigation_db (25/15/25/25 dB). This is
a decision-policy comparison, not two independently-realized countermeasure
systems (see docs/DECISIONS.md).

Interpretation: Rule-Based is effectively instant (a dB lookup); DQN costs a
few ms of real neural-network inference. This is the genuine speed trade-off
of using a learned policy over a static one in this system -- DQN's value is
not faster convergence (both converge in 1 step) but rather the potential to
learn better-than-rule-based action choices from data (measured separately as
recovery%% and agreement rate above).

Saved results/kpi3_measurement.txt
